### `sharegpt_3swiY8I_36`

- Facts: **32** · Keyphrases: **17** · Events: **1**

#### Facts

1. *(turn 0)* I said this is part 17 of my multiple messages.
2. *(turn 0)* I said the Master erred in the approach he adopted.
3. *(turn 0)* I said the integrity of the entire levee system is a matter of great importance to the town of Brewarrina.
4. *(turn 0)* I said a whole levee bank might be breached by water penetration around a culvert.
5. *(turn 0)* I said a levee might fail at any location where fill is sufficiently loose or cracks are sufficiently deep.
6. *(turn 0)* I said Professor Fell, an expert witness for the Council, testified that only one layer of poorly compacted soil might give a path for seepage and initiation of erosion.
7. *(turn 0)* I said the relevance of the contractual requirements for testing at the culverts is self-evident.
8. *(turn 0)* I said the purpose of the testing requirements was to give an appropriate degree of satisfaction to the Council as to the quality of the work done and the integrity of the system.
9. *(turn 0)* I accepted Beckhaus’s submission that failure to carry out some tests does not necessarily mean the completed works do not substantially comply with the contractual standard.
10. *(turn 0)* I said that once it is established there has been a failure to comply with the testing requirements, the evidentiary onus passes to Beckhaus to provide other evidence to the same degree of assurance as satisfactory test results would have provided.
11. *(turn 0)* I said the assurance could be derived from tests carried out subsequently or from some other source, and that Beckhaus would need to establish it.
12. *(turn 0)* I said Beckhaus failed to comply with the obligation to add 3% lime to all material.
13. *(turn 0)* I said the failure to add 3% lime gives rise to at least a prima facie doubt that the levees had the qualities that the addition of lime to that extent would achieve.
14. *(turn 0)* I said compaction tests known as “CETS” were performed while Beckhaus was carrying out work under the Contract in circumstances that cast some doubt on the conclusions from the results.
15. *(turn 0)* I said some 340 CETS tests were carried out in random places relating to compaction levels on the levee system.
16. *(turn 0)* I said all those 340 CETS tests passed.
17. *(turn 0)* I said Beckhaus submitted that the CETS results alone provided a high degree of assurance that contractual standards had been met generally.
18. *(turn 0)* I said some CETS tests only passed after the persons testing indicated to Beckhaus that a test at the particular place was likely to fail.
19. *(turn 0)* I said Beckhaus then added lime and reworked the area before the test was carried out and passed successfully.
20. *(turn 0)* I said Mr Rudge explained that the CETS testers did not issue failing certificates.
21. *(turn 0)* I said the CETS testers were sufficiently experienced to tell on site that something was not properly compacted because of deleterious material within the area and that it would fail.
22. *(turn 0)* I said the CETS testers would tell the foreman that the area was not going to pass, and the foreman would arrange for it to be reworked.
23. *(turn 0)* I said after reworking, an adjacent or nearby area would be retested.
24. *(turn 0)* I said if it looked as if it was going to pass, the sample was then taken back to the laboratory and generally it did.
25. *(turn 0)* I said this practice detracts from the degree of assurance that successful CETS testing would otherwise have provided.
26. *(turn 0)* I said these two matters emphasise the need for Beckhaus to rebut the inference arising from failure to provide satisfactory test results required by cl 3.8 and cl 8.9.3 of the specification.
27. *(turn 0)* I said the mere fact that compaction was actually performed at culverts does not provide the degree of assurance that would have been forthcoming had satisfactory tests been carried out at the frequency required by the Contract.
28. *(turn 0)* I said the Master erred in finding that the evidence demonstrated substantial compliance with the contractual requirements for compaction over the culverts.
29. *(turn 0)* I said the Council is entitled to damages flowing from breach of contract arising from failure to carry out tests required in relation to compaction at the culverts.
30. *(turn 0)* I said the damages are the costs of rectifying the work at the culverts.
31. *(turn 0)* I said rectification would involve removing the fill over and in the vicinity of the culverts sufficiently to enable the work to be done in accordance with the contract.
32. *(turn 0)* I proposed that the assessment of such damages should be remitted to Macready AsJ.

#### Keyphrases

`part 17 multiple messages`, `levee system integrity`, `town of Brewarrina`, `levee bank breach`, `culvert seepage erosion`, `contractual testing requirements`, `failure to carry out tests`, `evidentiary onus`, `Beckhaus submission`, `3% lime addition`, `prima facie doubt`, `CETS compaction tests`, `340 CETS tests`, `compaction over culverts`, `cl 3.8 and cl 8.9.3`, `damages for rectifying work`, `remitted to Macready AsJ`

### `sharegpt_4512LSp_0`

- Facts: **22** · Keyphrases: **12** · Events: **1**

#### Facts

1. *(turn 0)* I am working with a mock census dataset generated in Python using the Faker package.
2. *(turn 0)* The mock census dataset is designed to emulate the format of the 1881 census of the UK.
3. *(turn 0)* The dataset includes fields for Street Number, Street Name, First Name, Surname, Age, Relationship to the Head of House, Marital Status, Gender, Occupation, Infirmity, and Religion.
4. *(turn 0)* In the dataset, Street Number is set to "1" if it is a unique dwelling.
5. *(turn 0)* In the dataset, Relationship to the Head of House can be any person aged over 18, and the Head is the person responsible for filling in the census details.
6. *(turn 0)* In the dataset, Marital Status values are Single, Married, Divorced, Widowed, or "NA" for minors.
7. *(turn 0)* In the dataset, Gender values are Male or Female, and other responses were not implemented in 1881.
8. *(turn 0)* In the dataset, Occupation is implemented in a modern style rather than typical 1881 occupations.
9. *(turn 0)* In the dataset, Infirmity uses a limited set of infirmities following the style of 1881.
10. *(turn 0)* In the dataset, Religion uses a set of real-world religions.
11. *(turn 0)* I need to clean the dataset because it has missing entries and some responses that are outright lies.
12. *(turn 0)* Part of the grading for the assignment assesses the cleaning details.
13. *(turn 0)* The town in the census is modestly sized and is sandwiched between two much larger cities connected by motorways.
14. *(turn 0)* The town does not have a university, but students live in the town and commute to nearby cities.
15. *(turn 0)* After cleaning, I must decide what should be built on an unoccupied plot of land the local government wishes to develop.
16. *(turn 0)* One development choice is high-density housing if the population is significantly expanding.
17. *(turn 0)* One development choice is low-density housing if the population is affluent and there is demand for large family housing.
18. *(turn 0)* One development choice is a train station to take pressure off the roads by serving commuters.
19. *(turn 0)* One development choice is a religious building, considering demand for a second Church for Catholics or a different denomination or religious building.
20. *(turn 0)* One development choice is an emergency medical building (a minor injuries centre) if there are many injuries or likely future pregnancies.

26. *(turn 0)* I stated that the First World War led to the end of the Hapsburg empire.
27. *(turn 0)* I stated that the First World War unbalanced forever an enfeebled Europe.
28. *(turn 0)* I stated that the peaceful time of Joyce and Proust involved man grappling only with the monster of his own soul.
29. *(turn 0)* I stated that in the novels of Kafka, Hasek, Musil, and Broch, the monster comes from outside and is called History.
30. *(turn 0)* I stated that History in those novels is impersonal, uncontrollable, incalculable, incomprehensible, and inescapable.
31. *(turn 0)* I stated that just after the First World War, a group of great Central European novelists saw, felt, and grasped the terminal paradoxes of the Modern Era.

### `sharegpt_5NoXULS_0`

- Facts: **50** · Keyphrases: **12** · Events: **6**

#### Facts

1. *(turn 0)* I discussed bisphenol-A (BPA) as a chemical with hormone-like behavior that has been associated with health issues.
2. *(turn 0)* BPA is a component of polycarbonate plastic.
3. *(turn 0)* BPA is used as an additive in some epoxy resins and other materials.
4. *(turn 0)* BPA has been removed or substituted in many cases, especially in water bottles and baby bottles.
5. *(turn 0)* A long review by the European Food Safety Agency (EFSA) was mentioned as reinforcing concerns and findings.
6. *(turn 0)* The EFSA review concluded that BPA has the potential to cause harm at high levels, but most exposures are so low that risks are negligible based on current understanding.
7. *(turn 0)* The discussion focused on BPA exposure from contaminated food as the most relevant source of exposure in most studies.
8. *(turn 0)* Ingested BPA is not readily absorbed from the gut.
9. *(turn 0)* When ingested, BPA is rapidly converted (metabolized) into a less harmful form and removed in healthy adults.
10. *(turn 0)* BPA can potentially get into the body through the skin.
11. *(turn 0)* A team at the University of Missouri was particularly interested in BPA exposure through skin.
12. *(turn 0)* BPA is used as a developer for heat-sensitive ink in many cash register receipts that use thermal paper.
13. *(turn 0)* BPA is applied as a surface coating on thermal paper receipts.
14. *(turn 0)* BPA can potentially transfer from receipts to hands during handling.
15. *(turn 0)* The study tested whether hand sanitizer could dissolve BPA from receipts and enhance absorption through the skin.
16. *(turn 0)* The study measured BPA present in cash register receipts from local retailers.
17. *(turn 0)* The study found an average of 20 mg of BPA per receipt in receipts that used BPA.
18. *(turn 0)* The study found that BPA was 2% of the weight of those receipts.
19. *(turn 0)* The study tested volunteers with dry hands handling receipts for different lengths of time up to 6 minutes.
20. *(turn 0)* After 6 minutes of handling a receipt with dry hands, an average of 7 µg of BPA ended up on a person’s hand.
21. *(turn 0)* After 6 minutes, the 7 µg of BPA on the hand was about 0.08% of the BPA on the receipt.
22. *(turn 0)* The study tested what happened when hands were coated with Purell and a receipt was held without allowing the sanitizer to dry.
23. *(turn 0)* With Purell-coated wet hands, the study found a maximum of 600 µg of BPA transferred after one minute.
24. *(turn 0)* With Purell-coated wet hands, about 7% of the BPA on the receipt ended up on the hand after one minute.
25. *(turn 0)* The study measured how much BPA passed through the skin and into the bloodstream.
26. *(turn 0)* Volunteers held a BPA-coated receipt for four minutes with the BPA-coated side in contact with the hand.
27. *(turn 0)* Blood samples were taken for 90 minutes.
28. *(turn 0)* The analysis looked at free (unconjugated) BPA in blood.
29. *(turn 0)* The analysis looked at conjugated BPA in blood.
30. *(turn 0)* The study had volunteers eat 10 french fries from their Purell and BPA-coated hands.
31. *(turn 0)* The study found measurable quantities of unconjugated BPA enter the bloodstream within minutes after holding a BPA-containing receipt for 4 minutes with a Purell-coated hand.
32. *(turn 0)* The peak blood concentration of unconjugated BPA was 5.7 nanograms per milliliter for males.
33. *(turn 0)* The peak blood concentration of unconjugated BPA was 10.2 nanograms per milliliter for females.
34. *(turn 0)* For females, the discussion stated that around ten billionths of a blood sample would be BPA at the peak concentration.
35. *(turn 0)* For volunteers who did not use Purell, there was no measurable increase in BPA in their blood.
36. *(turn 0)* The discussion suggested most BPA found in blood samples was from the skin rather than from transferred BPA entering the gut.
37. *(turn 0)* The discussion speculated that BPA on the fries could have passed into the bloodstream through the inside of the mouth.
38. *(turn 0)* The discussion stated that the combination of hand sanitizer and BPA-containing cash register receipts can lead to elevated levels of unconjugated BPA in the blood stream.
39. *(turn 0)* The discussion stated that handling receipts with clean, dry hands is unlikely to lead to significant transfer of BPA from receipts to the blood.
40. *(turn 0)* The discussion said the Purell exposure scenarios were not realistic for most people.
41. *(turn 0)* The discussion said holding receipts in Purell-coated hands with the printed side palm-down for up to four minutes was not likely to be routine.
42. *(turn 0)* The discussion said the measured blood levels are peak levels from one-off events and are small.
43. *(turn 0)* The discussion said risk interpretation would require information about how often such events occur over a day.
44. *(turn 0)* The discussion emphasized that dose over time matters more than a one-off peak.
45. *(turn 0)* The discussion said unconjugated BPA levels were relatively high compared to conjugated BPA.
46. *(turn 0)* The discussion suggested unconjugated BPA may be higher because BPA passes through the skin and enters the bloodstream before passing through the liver.
47. *(turn 0)* The discussion stated that the authors suggested increased risk to sensitive organs and systems, but that this remains speculative without further research.
48. *(turn 0)* The discussion concluded that the speaker would not change cash register receipt habits.
49. *(turn 0)* The discussion suggested that smothering hands with Purell and clutching receipts for several minutes probably isn’t advisable.
50. *(turn 0)* The discussion specifically warned against doing this especially if about to eat french fries.

#### Keyphrases

`bisphenol-A BPA`, `polycarbonate plastic`, `epoxy resins`, `EFSA review`, `thermal paper receipts`, `cash register receipts`, `heat-sensitive ink`, `hand sanitizer Purell`, `BPA skin absorption`, `unconjugated BPA uBPA`, `blood peak concentration`, `french fries exposure`

#### Events

1. *(turn 0 · date_hint: `earlier in the year`)* Mention of a long EFSA review earlier in the year
2. *(turn 0 · date_hint: `up to 6 minutes`)* Experiment measuring BPA transfer from receipts to hands with dry hands up to 6 minutes
3. *(turn 0 · date_hint: `one minute`)* Experiment measuring BPA transfer from receipts to Purell-coated wet hands after one minute
4. *(turn 0 · date_hint: `four minutes`)* Volunteers held a BPA-coated receipt for four minutes with the receipt side in contact with the hand
5. *(turn 0 · date_hint: `90 minutes`)* Blood samples were taken for 90 minutes after receipt contact
6. *(turn 0 · date_hint: `during the study`)* Volunteers ate 10 french fries from their Purell and BPA-coated hands

#### Facts

1. *(turn 0)* I wanted to love the toaster but it doesn’t toast evenly.
2. *(turn 0)* I bought a simple 4-slice toaster in 2009.
3. *(turn 0)* I expected this toaster to have a bagel feature but it doesn’t.
4. *(turn 0)* I found that the toaster cannot accommodate four slices.
5. *(turn 0)* When I make breakfast for the family, I toast two slices at a time.
6. *(turn 0)* Some spots on the toast are nearly burnt while others are barely toasted.
7. *(turn 0)* When I toast English muffins, setting 3 is too light and setting 4 is burnt.
8. *(turn 0)* Because the toaster is digital, there is no in-between setting.
9. *(turn 0)* I read the included book and put the slices in the center as it states for two slices, but I still got bad results.
10. *(turn 0)* I am going to donate this toaster and purchase a different brand.
11. *(turn 0)* I found that the toaster does not toast the bread near the top of the slice as much as the remainder of the slice.
12. *(turn 0)* I think the digital deal is neat but it leaves toasting on 2 and then toasting a bit more.
13. *(turn 0)* I found that a 3 setting makes toast dark and dry.
14. *(turn 0)* I prefer the dial type controller on older toasters because it lets me dial in the right amount of time.
15. *(turn 0)* I think the toaster looks nice and is easy to operate.
16. *(turn 0)* I have to baby sit my toast with this toaster.
17. *(turn 0)* I was taken aback by how large but lightweight/flimsy the toaster felt upon receiving it.
18. *(turn 0)* I recommend measuring the space before buying because the toaster gets super hot at least 12 inches above it under an angled roofline.
19. *(turn 0)* I need to pull the toaster forward so the ceiling doesn’t get over hot.
20. *(turn 0)* The sides of the toaster get really hot.
21. *(turn 0)* The toaster cord is not super long.

23. *(turn 0)* The cord comes out near-ish the plunger.
24. *(turn 0)* The toaster eject is jarring and loud.
25. *(turn 0)* The first time, my cat flew straight into the air and then ripped out of the room.
26. *(turn 0)* Over time, the eject seems a bit less jarring.
27. *(turn 0)* The first time I used it, I followed the recommendation to run it without bread to burn off the initial strong chemical smell.
28. *(turn 0)* I think the weight of bread may slow the eject down.
29. *(turn 0)* I would not necessarily recommend the toaster but I will keep it.
30. *(turn 0)* My next toaster will probably be the America’s Test Kitchen highly-rated $100 Breville.
31. *(turn 0)* This toaster cost $35 (or $40 minus a $5 coupon).
32. *(turn 0)* I like the ability to toast 2 long slices (artisanal sourdough) or 4 English muffins at a time.
33. *(turn 0)* I appreciate the wide slots for specialty bakery breads that wouldn’t fit in a normal toaster.
34. *(turn 0)* I appreciate the ability to cook four slices of standard bread at once.
35. *(turn 0)* I like the timing function for trying to get all parts of the meal ready at the same time.
36. *(turn 0)* I found the toaster doesn’t have a deep enough well and/or the toaster elements don’t come up high enough, so the top of the bread doesn’t get toasted.
37. *(turn 0)* I have to turn the bread over and toast it longer on that side.
38. *(turn 0)* When I do that, the middle of the bread gets too well done.
39. *(turn 0)* I like my toast fairly well done but I hate hard toast.
40. *(turn 0)* I want a one-and-done toasting situation.
41. *(turn 0)* I found the clean-out tray at the bottom never seems to have much on it when I empty it.
42. *(turn 0)* I found the toaster spews crumbs and seeds all over the counter even when the clean-up tray is inserted.
43. *(turn 0)* I’m glad I didn’t pay much for this toaster because the cons outweigh the pros.
